{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-5-20251101","id":"msg_vrtx_016MdusiYFgSVTnCMNeYCHzd","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01RcekRtC1K4h2tdaJjbYXYS","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/app/environment/skills/atheris-python-fuzzing.md","content":"# Atheris: Python Coverage-Guided Fuzzing\n\n## Overview\n\nAtheris is a coverage-guided Python fuzzing engine developed by Google. It is based on libFuzzer and provides native Python fuzzing capabilities with coverage feedback.\n\n## Installation\n\n```bash\npip install atheris\n```\n\n## Basic Usage\n\n### Minimal Fuzz Target\n\n```python\nimport atheris\nimport sys\n\ndef TestOneInput(data):\n    \"\"\"Fuzz target function that receives random bytes.\"\"\"\n    if len(data) < 5:\n        return\n    # Parse data and test your library\n    try:\n        # Your code under test\n        pass\n    except Exception:\n        pass  # Expected exceptions should be caught\n\natheris.Setup(sys.argv, TestOneInput)\natheris.Fuzz()\n```\n\n### Using FuzzedDataProvider\n\n`FuzzedDataProvider` helps convert raw bytes into typed data:\n\n```python\nimport atheris\nimport sys\n\nwith atheris.instrument_imports():\n    import target_library\n\ndef TestOneInput(data):\n    fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n\n    # Generate typed data from fuzz input\n    string = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(fdp.ConsumeIntInRange(0, 100))\n    integer = fdp.ConsumeInt(4)  # 4-byte integer\n    boolean = fdp.ConsumeBool()\n    float_val = fdp.ConsumeFloat()\n\n    try:\n        target_library.function(string, integer)\n    except (ValueError, TypeError):\n        pass  # Expected exceptions\n\natheris.Setup(sys.argv, TestOneInput)\natheris.Fuzz()\n```\n\n### FuzzedDataProvider Methods\n\n| Method | Description |\n|--------|-------------|\n| `ConsumeBytes(n)` | Consume n bytes |\n| `ConsumeUnicode(n)` | Consume n unicode characters |\n| `ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(n)` | Unicode without surrogate characters |\n| `ConsumeString(n)` | Consume n-byte string |\n| `ConsumeInt(n)` | Consume n-byte integer |\n| `ConsumeIntInRange(min, max)` | Integer in range |\n| `ConsumeFloat()` | Consume a float |\n| `ConsumeFloatInRange(min, max)` | Float in range |\n| `ConsumeBool()` | Consume a boolean |\n| `ConsumeIntList(n, byte_size)` | List of n integers |\n| `ConsumeFloatList(n)` | List of n floats |\n| `PickValueInList(lst)` | Pick random element from list |\n| `ConsumeRemainingBytes()` | Get all remaining bytes |\n| `ConsumeRemainingString()` | Get remaining as string |\n\n## Instrumenting Code\n\nFor coverage-guided fuzzing, instrument the target module:\n\n```python\nimport atheris\n\n# Instrument imports BEFORE importing target\nwith atheris.instrument_imports():\n    import my_target_library\n```\n\nOr instrument specific modules:\n\n```python\nimport atheris\nimport my_module\n\natheris.instrument_all()  # Instrument all loaded modules\n# OR\natheris.instrument_func(my_module.specific_function)\n```\n\n## Running the Fuzzer\n\n### Basic Run\n```bash\npython fuzz.py\n```\n\n### With Time Limit\n```bash\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=10  # Run for 10 seconds\n```\n\n### With Corpus Directory\n```bash\npython fuzz.py corpus/\n```\n\n### Common LibFuzzer Options\n- `-max_total_time=N` - Run for N seconds\n- `-max_len=N` - Maximum input length\n- `-timeout=N` - Timeout per test case\n- `-jobs=N` - Number of parallel jobs\n- `-workers=N` - Number of workers\n- `-dict=file` - Use dictionary file\n- `-seed=N` - Random seed\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Catch Expected Exceptions**: Always catch exceptions that are expected behavior\n2. **Use FuzzedDataProvider**: Convert raw bytes to meaningful types\n3. **Instrument Target Code**: Use `instrument_imports()` for coverage\n4. **Minimize Input Size**: Check for minimum required input length\n5. **Test Edge Cases**: Focus on parsing, serialization, and validation code\n\n## Example: Fuzzing JSON Parser\n\n```python\nimport atheris\nimport sys\n\nwith atheris.instrument_imports():\n    import json\n\ndef TestOneInput(data):\n    fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n    json_str = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(fdp.ConsumeIntInRange(0, 1000))\n\n    try:\n        json.loads(json_str)\n    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError):\n        pass\n\natheris.Setup(sys.argv, TestOneInput)\natheris.Fuzz()\n```\n\n## Logging Output\n\nTo capture fuzzing logs:\n\n```bash\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=10 2>&1 | tee fuzz.log\n```\n\nOr redirect stderr (where libFuzzer outputs):\n\n```bash\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=10 2>fuzz.log\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":344,"cache_read_input_tokens":19316,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":344,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"4da5c0e5-ebe3-4438-80d5-94ced58e0502","uuid":"763352ca-4b09-42e9-9b54-da5456dd5ef5"}

This skill covers creating and using venvs for library development and testing.\n\n## Creating a Virtual Environment\n\n### Using venv (Built-in)\n\n```bash\n# Create venv in project directory\npython3 -m venv /path/to/project/.venv\n\n# Activate the venv\nsource /path/to/project/.venv/bin/activate\n\n# Deactivate when done\ndeactivate\n```\n\n## Installing Dependencies\n\n### From requirements.txt\n\n```bash\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npip install -r requirements.txt\n```\n\n### From pyproject.toml (Modern Python Projects)\n\n```bash\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npip install .  # Install the package itself\npip install -e .  # Install in editable/development mode\npip install -e \".[dev]\"  # Install with dev dependencies\npip install -e \".[test]\"  # Install with test dependencies\n```\n\n### From setup.py (Legacy)\n\n```bash\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npip install -e .\n```\n\n## Common Dependency Files\n\n| File | Description |\n|------|-------------|\n| `requirements.txt` | Simple pip requirements |\n| `requirements-dev.txt` | Development dependencies |\n| `pyproject.toml` | Modern Python packaging (PEP 517/518) |\n| `setup.py` | Legacy packaging script |\n| `setup.cfg` | Legacy declarative config |\n| `Pipfile` | Pipenv format |\n| `poetry.lock` | Poetry format |\n\n## Reading pyproject.toml Dependencies\n\n```toml\n[project]\ndependencies = [\n    \"requests>=2.28.0\",\n    \"click>=8.0.0\",\n]\n\n[project.optional-dependencies]\ndev = [\"pytest\", \"black\", \"mypy\"]\ntest = [\"pytest\", \"pytest-cov\"]\n```\n\nTo install:\n```bash\npip install -e \".[dev,test]\"\n```\n\n## Running Commands in Venv\n\n### Without Activating\n\n```bash\n/path/to/.venv/bin/python script.py\n/path/to/.venv/bin/pip install package\n```\n\n### With Activation\n\n```bash\nsource /path/to/.venv/bin/activate\npython script.py\npip install package\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Use project-local venv**: Create `.venv` in project root\n2. **Add to .gitignore**: Exclude `.venv/` from version control\n3. **Document requirements**: Keep requirements files updated\n4. **Use editable installs**: `-e .` for development\n\n## Example: Setting Up a Library for Fuzzing\n\n```bash\n# Navigate to library\ncd /app/my_library\n\n# Create venv\npython3 -m venv .venv\n\n# Activate\nsource .venv/bin/activate\n\n# Install library This skill covers creating and using venvs for library development and testing.\n\n## Creating a Virtual Environment\n\n### Using venv (Built-in)\n\n```bash\n# Create venv in project directory\npython3 -m venv /path/to/project/.venv\n\n# Activate the venv\nsource /path/to/project/.venv/bin/activate\n\n# Deactivate when done\ndeactivate\n```\n\n## Installing Dependencies\n\n### From requirements.txt\n\n```bash\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npip install -r requirements.txt\n```\n\n### From pyproject.toml (Modern Python Projects)\n\n```bash\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npip install .  # Install the package itself\npip install -e .  # Install in editable/development mode\npip install -e \".[dev]\"  # Install with dev dependencies\npip install -e \".[test]\"  # Install with test dependencies\n```\n\n### From setup.py (Legacy)\n\n```bash\nsource .venv/bin/activate\npip install -e .\n```\n\n## Common Dependency Files\n\n| File | Description |\n|------|-------------|\n| `requirements.txt` | Simple pip requirements |\n| `requirements-dev.txt` | Development dependencies |\n| `pyproject.toml` | Modern Python packaging (PEP 517/518) |\n| `setup.py` | Legacy packaging script |\n| `setup.cfg` | Legacy declarative config |\n| `Pipfile` | Pipenv format |\n| `poetry.lock` | Poetry format |\n\n## Reading pyproject.toml Dependencies\n\n```toml\n[project]\ndependencies = [\n    \"requests>=2.28.0\",\n    \"click>=8.0.0\",\n]\n\n[project.optional-dependencies]\ndev = [\"pytest\", \"black\", \"mypy\"]\ntest = [\"pytest\", \"pytest-cov\"]\n```\n\nTo install:\n```bash\npip install -e \".[dev,test]\"\n```\n\n## Running Commands in Venv\n\n### Without Activating\n\n```bash\n/path/to/.venv/bin/python script.py\n/path/to/.venv/bin/pip install package\n```\n\n### With Activation\n\n```bash\nsource /path/to/.venv/bin/activate\npython script.py\npip install package\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Use project-local venv**: Create `.venv` in project root\n2. **Add to .gitignore**: Exclude `.venv/` from version control\n3. **Document requirements**: Keep requirements files updated\n4. **Use editable installs**: `-e .` for development\n\n## Example: Setting Up a Library for Fuzzing\n\n```bash\n# Navigate to library\ncd /app/my_library\n\n# Create venv\npython3 -m venv .venv\n\n# Activate\nsource .venv/bin/activate\n\n# Install library and dependencies\npip install -e .\n\n# Install fuzzing tools\npip install atheris\n\n# Run fuzzer\npython fuzz.py\n```\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Call target function\n        target_library.function(fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(100))\n    except (ValueError, TypeError, KeyError):\n        # Expected exceptions - don't report as bugs\n        pass\n\ndef main():\n    atheris.Setup(sys.argv, TestOneInput)\n    atheris.Fuzz()\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n```\n\n## Pattern: String Parsing Functions\n\n```python\ndef TestOneInput(data: bytes) -> None:\n    fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n\n    # Generate string input\n    input_str = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(\n        fdp.ConsumeIntInRange(0, 500)\n    )\n\n    try:\n        library.parse(input_str)\n    except (ValueError, library.ParseError):\n        pass\n```\n\n## Pattern: JSON/Dict Processing\n\n```python\ndef TestOneInput(data: bytes) -> None:\n    fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n\n    # Try to parse as JSON-like structure\n    try:\n        input_str = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(\n            fdp.ConsumeIntInRange(0, 1000)\n        )\n        library.process_json(input_str)\n    except (json.JSONDecodeError, ValueError, TypeError, KeyError):\n        pass\n```\n\n## Pattern: Binary/Bytes Processing\n\n```python\ndef TestOneInput(data: bytes) -> None:\n    if len(data) < 4:\n        return\n\n    try:\n        library.decode(data)\n    except (ValueError, UnicodeDecodeError, struct.error):\n        pass\n```\n\n## Pattern: Multiple Function Targets\n\n```python\ndef TestOneInput(data: bytes) -> None:\n    fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n\n    # Choose which function to test\n    choice = fdp.ConsumeIntInRange(0, 2)\n    input_data = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(100)\n\n    try:\n        if choice == 0:\n            library.parse(input_data)\n        elif choice == 1:\n            library.format(input_data)\n        else:\n            library.validate(input_data)\n    except (ValueError, TypeError):\n        pass\n```\n\n## Pattern: Class Instance Testing\n\n```python\ndef TestOneInput(data: bytes) -> None:\n    fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n\n    try:\n        # Create instance with fuzzed data\n        obj = library.MyClass(\n            name=fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(50),\n            value=fdp.ConsumeInt(4)\n        )\n        # Test methods\n        obj.process()\n        str(obj)\n        repr(obj)\n    except (ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError):\n        pass\n```\n\n## Pattern: Date/Time Libraries\n\n```python\ndef TestOneInput(data: bytes) -> None:\n    fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n\n    date_str = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(\n        fdp.ConsumeIntInRange(0, 100)\n    )\n    format_str = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(\n        fdp.ConsumeIntInRange(0, 50)\n    )\n\n    try:\n        library.parse(date_str, format=format_str)\n    except (ValueError, TypeError, OverflowError):\n        pass\n```\n\n## Pattern: Code Formatting Libraries\n\n```python\ndef TestOneInput(data: bytes) -> None:\n    fdp = atheris.FuzzedDataProvider(data)\n\n    # Generate Python-like code\n    code = fdp.ConsumeUnicodeNoSurrogates(\n        fdp.ConsumeIntInRange(0, 2000)\n    )\n\n    try:\n        library.format_code(code)\n    except (SyntaxError, ValueError, library.FormatError):\n        pass\n```\n\n## Exception Handling Guidelines\n\n### Catch Expected Exceptions\n\n```python\n# Common expected exceptions\nexcept (\n    ValueError,      # Invalid values\n    TypeError,       # Wrong types\n    KeyError,        # Missing keys\n    IndexError,      # Out of bounds\n    AttributeError,  # Missing attributes\n    UnicodeDecodeError,  # Encoding issues\n    OverflowError,   # Numeric overflow\n    RecursionError,  # Too deep recursion\n):\n    pass\n```\n\n### Don't Catch\n\n- `MemoryError` - May indicate real issue\n- `SystemExit` - Should not occur\n- `KeyboardInterrupt` - User interrupt\n- Assertion errors (unless expected)\n\n## Running the Fuzzer\n\n```bash\n# Basic run\npython fuzz.py\n\n# Time-limited run (10 seconds)\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=10\n\n# With log redirection\npython fuzz.py -max_total_time=10 2>&1 | tee fuzz.log\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":983,"cache_read_input_tokens":21932,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":983,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"4da5c0e5-ebe3-4438-80d5-94ced58e0502","uuid":"003e8c98-aa45-46ef-997a-02ef36e68d37"}

1336→def get_features_used(\n  1337→    node: Node, *, future_imports: set[str] | None = None\n  1338→) -> set[Feature]:\n  1339→    \"\"\"Return a set of (relatively) new Python features used in this self-documenting expressions in f-strings (f\"{x=}\");\n    - underscores in numeric literals;\n    - trailing commas after * or ** in function signatures and calls;\n    - positional only arguments in function signatures and lambdas;\n    - assignment expression;\n    - relaxed decorator syntax;\n    - usage of __future__ flags (annotations);\n    - print / exec statements;\n    - parenthesized context managers;\n    - match statements;\n    - except* clause;\n    - variadic generics;\n    \"\"\"\n    features: set[Feature] = set()\n    if future_imports:\n        features |= {\n n.type == token.RBRACE\n            and n.parent is not None\n            and any(child.type == token.EQUAL for child in n.parent.children)\n        ):\n token.SLASH:\n            if n.parent and n.parent.type in {\n                syms.typedargslist,\n                syms.arglist,\n                syms.varargslist,\n            }:\n                features.add(Feature.POS_ONLY_ARGUMENTS)\n\n        elif n.type == token.COLONEQUAL:\n            features.add(Feature.ASSIGNMENT_EXPRESSIONS)\n\n        elif n.type == syms.decorator:\n            if len(n.children) > 1 and not is_simple_decorator_expression(\n                n.children[1]\n            ):\n                features.add(Feature.RELAXED_DECORATORS)\n\n        elif (\n feature = Feature.TRAILING_COMMA_IN_DEF\n            else:\n                feature = Feature.TRAILING_COMMA_IN_CALL\n\n            for ch in n.children:\n                if ch.type in STARS:\n                    features.add(feature)\n\n                if ch.type == syms.argument:\n                    for argch in ch.children:\n                        if argch.type in STARS:\n                            features.add(feature)\n\n        elif (\n            n.type in {syms.return_stmt, syms.yield_expr}\n            and len(n.children) >= 2\n            and n.children[1].type == == syms.match_stmt:\n            features.add(Feature.PATTERN_MATCHING)\n\n        elif n.type in {syms.subscriptlist, syms.trailer} and any(\n            child.type == syms.star_expr for child in features.add(Feature.TYPE_PARAMS)\n\n        elif (\n            n.type in (syms.typevartuple, syms.paramspec, syms.typevar)\n            and n.children[-2].type == token.EQUAL\n        ):\n features.add(Feature.EXCEPT_STAR)\n\n            # Presence of except* pushes as clause 1 index back\n            has_as_clause = (\n                len(n.children) >= is_star_except + 3\n {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_vrtx_01Htn6nLYW2rxmNTQiVQ7XA6","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# UltraJSON\n     2→\n     3→[![PyPI version](https://img.shields.io/pypi/v/ujson.svg?logo=pypi&logoColor=FFE873)](https://pypi.org/project/ujson)\n     4→[![Supported Python versions](https://img.shields.io/pypi/pyversions/ujson.svg?logo=python&logoColor=FFE873)](https://pypi.org/project/ujson)\n     5→[![PyPI downloads](https://img.shields.io/pypi/dm/ujson.svg)](https://pypistats.org/packages/ujson)\n     6→[![GitHub Actions status](https://github.com/ultrajson/ultrajson/workflows/Test/badge.svg)](https://github.com/ultrajson/ultrajson/actions)\n     7→[![codecov](https://codecov.io/gh/ultrajson/ultrajson/branch/main/graph/badge.svg)](https://codecov.io/gh/ultrajson/ultrajson)\n     8→[![DOI](https://zenodo.org/badge/1418941.svg)](https://zenodo.org/badge/latestdoi/1418941)\n     9→[![Code style: Black](https://img.shields.io/badge/code%20style-Black-000000.svg)](https://github.com/psf/black)\n    10→\n    11→UltraJSON is an ultra fast JSON encoder and decoder written in pure C with bindings for\n    12→Python.\n    13→\n    14→Install with pip:\n    15→\n    16→```sh\n    17→python -m pip install ujson\n    18→```\n    19→\n    20→## Project status\n    21→\n    22→> [!WARNING]\n    23→> UltraJSON's architecture is fundamentally ill-suited to making changes without\n    24→> risk of introducing new security vulnerabilities. As a result, this library\n    25→> has been put into a *maintenance-only* mode. Support for new Python versions\n    26→> will be added and critical bugs and security issues will still be\n    27→> fixed but all other changes will be rejected. Users are encouraged to migrate\n    28→> to [orjson](https://pypi.org/project/orjson/) which is both much faster and\n    29→> less likely to introduce a surprise buffer overflow vulnerability in the\n    30→> future.\n    31→\n    32→## Usage\n    33→\n    34→May be used as a drop in replacement for most other JSON parsers for Python:\n    35→\n    36→```pycon\n    37→>>> import ujson\n    38→>>> ujson.dumps([{\"key\": \"value\"}, 81, True])\n    39→'[{\"key\":\"value\"},81,true]'\n    40→>>> ujson.loads(\"\"\"[{\"key\": \"value\"}, 81, true]\"\"\")\n    41→[{'key': 'value'}, 81, True]\n    42→```\n    43→\n    44→### Encoder options\n    45→\n    46→#### encode_html_chars\n    47→\n    48→Used to enable special encoding of \"unsafe\" HTML characters into safer Unicode\n    49→sequences. Default is `False`:\n    50→\n    51→```pycon\n    52→>>> ujson.dumps(\"<script>John&Doe\", encode_html_chars=True)\n    53→'\"\\\\u003cscript\\\\u003eJohn\\\\u0026Doe\"'\n    54→```\n    55→\n    56→#### ensure_ascii\n    57→\n    58→Limits output to ASCII and escapes all extended characters above 127. Default is `True`.\n    59→If your end format supports UTF-8, setting this option to false is highly recommended to\n    60→save space:\n    61→\n    62→```pycon\n    63→>>> ujson.dumps(\"åäö\")\n    64→'\"\\\\u00e5\\\\u00e4\\\\u00f6\"'\n    65→>>> ujson.dumps(\"åäö\", ensure_ascii=False)\n    66→'\"åäö\"'\n    67→```\n    68→\n    69→#### escape_forward_slashes\n    70→\n    71→Controls whether forward slashes (`/`) are escaped. Default is `True`:\n    72→\n    73→```pycon\n    74→>>> ujson.dumps(\"https://example.com\")\n    75→'\"https:\\\\/\\\\/example.com\"'\n    76→>>> ujson.dumps(\"https://example.com\", escape_forward_slashes=False)\n    77→'\"https://example.com\"'\n    78→```\n    79→\n    80→#### indent\n    81→\n    82→Controls whether indentation (\"pretty output\") is enabled. As a result, this library\n> has been put into a *maintenance-only* mode. Support for new Python versions\n> will be added and critical bugs and security issues will still be\n> fixed but all other changes will be rejected. Users are encouraged to migrate\n> to [orjson](https://pypi.org/project/orjson/) which is both much faster and\n> less likely to introduce a surprise buffer overflow vulnerability in the\n> future.\n\n## Usage\n\nMay be used as a drop in replacement for most other JSON parsers for Python:\n\n```pycon\n>>> import ujson\n>>> ujson.dumps([{\"key\": \"value\"}, 81, True])\n'[{\"key\":\"value\"},81,true]'\n>>> ujson.loads(\"\"\"[{\"key\": \"value\"}, 81, true]\"\"\")\n[{'key': 'value'}, 81, True]\n```\n\n### Encoder options\n\n#### encode_html_chars\n\nUsed to enable special encoding of \"unsafe\" HTML characters into safer Unicode\nsequences. Default is `False`:\n\n```pycon\n>>> ujson.dumps(\"<script>John&Doe\", encode_html_chars=True)\n'\"\\\\u003cscript\\\\u003eJohn\\\\u0026Doe\"'\n```\n\n#### ensure_ascii\n\nLimits output to ASCII and escapes all extended characters above 127. Default is `True`.\nIf your end format supports UTF-8, setting this option to false is highly recommended to\nsave space:\n\n```pycon\n>>> ujson.dumps(\"åäö\")\n'\"\\\\u00e5\\\\u00e4\\\\u00f6\"'\n>>> ujson.dumps(\"åäö\", ensure_ascii=False)\n'\"åäö\"'\n```\n\n#### escape_forward_slashes\n\nControls whether forward slashes (`/`) are escaped. Default is `True`:\n\n```pycon\n>>> ujson.dumps(\"https://example.com\")\n'\"https:\\\\/\\\\/example.com\"'\n>>> ujson.dumps(\"https://example.com\", escape_forward_slashes=False)\n'\"https://example.com\"'\n```\n\n#### indent\n\nControls whether indentation (\"pretty output\") is enabled. Default is `0` (disabled):\n\n```pycon\n>>> ujson.dumps({\"foo\": \"bar\"})\n'{\"foo\":\"bar\"}'\n>>> print(ujson.dumps({\"foo\": \"bar\"}, indent=4))\n{\n    \"foo\": \"bar\"\n}\n```\n\n## Benchmarks\n\n*UltraJSON* calls/sec compared to other popular JSON parsers with performance gain\nspecified below each.\n\n### Test machine\n\nLinux 5.15.0-1037-azure x86_64 #44-Ubuntu SMP Thu Apr 20 13:19:31 UTC 2023","numLines":100,"startLine":1,"totalLines":176}}}

if end_line >= len(lines):\n            break\n    return end_line\n\ndef assemble_continued_line(lines, start: Tuple[int, int], end_line: int):\n    r\"\"\"Assemble a single line from multiple continued line pieces\n\n    Continued lines are lines ending in ``\\``, and the line following the last\n    ``\\`` in the block.\n\n    For example, this code continues over multiple lines::\n\n        if (assign_ix is not None) \\\n             and (len(line) >= assign_ix + 2) \\\n             and (line[assign_ix+1].string == '%') \\\n             and (line[assign_ix+2].type == tokenize.NAME):\n\n    This statement contains four continued line pieces.\n    Assembling these pieces into a single line would give::\n\n        if (assign_ix is not None) and (len(line) >= assign_ix + 2) and (line[...\n\n    This uses 0-indexed line numbers. *start* is (lineno, colno).\n\n    Used to allow ``%magic`` and ``!system`` commands to be continued over\n    multiple lines.\n    \"\"\"\n    parts = [lines[start[0]][start[1]:]] + lines[start[0]+1:end_line+1]\n    return ' '.join([p.rstrip()[:-1] for p in parts[:-1]]  # Strip backslash+newline\n                    + [parts[-1].rstrip()])         # Strip newline from last line\n\nclass TokenTransformBase:\n    \"\"\"Base class for transformations which examine tokens.\n\n    Special syntax should not be transformed when it occurs inside strings or\n    comments. This is hard to reliably avoid with regexes. The solution is to\n    tokenise the code as Python, and recognise the special syntax in the tokens.\n\n    IPython's special syntax is not valid Python syntax, so tokenising may go\n    wrong after the special syntax starts. These classes therefore find and\n    transform *one* instance of special syntax at a time into regular Python\n    syntax. After each transformation, tokens are regenerated to find the next\n    piece of special syntax.\n\n    Subclasses need to implement one class method (find)\n    and one regular method (transform).\n\n    The priority attribute can select which transformation to apply if multiple\n    transformers match in the same place. Lower numbers have higher priority.","numLines":200,"startLine":1,"totalLines":809}}}
